# Rand Terminal Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Build randterminal.org: a Remix-style web IDE that turns pasted Solidity or Solana Rust into an RV32IM image through our transpilers and shows the run on the Rand zkVM, cycle by cycle, with the STARK tables it fills.

**Architecture:** A Rust axum service (`terminal-api`) links `evm2rv`, `sbpf2rv` and `rand_zkvm` in-process for analysis, transpilation and emulation, and runs the three untrusted tool steps (solc, cargo build-sbf, rand-guest build) in a locked-down Docker container. A Next.js app (`web`) talks only to that service. Every job lives in a directory on disk; every stage writes a JSON view the API serves verbatim; traces are compacted once into fixed-width files the debugger pages through.

**Tech Stack:** Rust 1.98.1, axum 0.7, tokio 1, tower-http 0.5, thiserror 1, serde 1, hex 0.4, bs58 0.5; circuits crates by path (`evm2rv`, `sbpf2rv`, `rand_zkvm`, `rand-guest`, `evm-core`, `sbpf-core`); Docker; Next 15.5 (App Router), React 19, TypeScript 5.9, Tailwind 4 via `@tailwindcss/postcss`, CodeMirror 6 (`codemirror` 6.0, `@codemirror/lang-rust` 6.0, `@replit/codemirror-lang-solidity` 6.0), vitest 5, Playwright 1.64.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-terminal-design.md` (this repo). Read it first; this plan argues from it.

## Global Constraints

- Rust toolchain **1.98.1** everywhere (`api/rust-toolchain.toml`); clang **23.1.1** in the sandbox image (the `hc` of every image depends on it); `cc` crate **1.4.6** is pinned by the generated shim crates (do not touch).
- `circuits` is a git submodule pinned at **`9bf3844`**; every path dependency points inside it (`../../../circuits/...` from `api/crates/<crate>`). Never edit files under `circuits/`.
- solc **0.8.37** only, flags `--optimize --optimize-runs 200 --evm-version shanghai`; Linux static binary sha256 `5de843c2c93563cc66425c99a4fb13fdbf32b4c4ae07469480faaf126e14404a`.
- Solana platform-tools **v1.54** (`cargo build-sbf --tools-version v1.54`), agave release **v4.3.0** for the `cargo-build-sbf` binary.
- Default `chain_id` **20**; default program-word cap **65 535** (`TERMINAL_MAX_PROGRAM_WORDS`).
- Limits: source files ≤ **512 KB** total, `.so` ≤ **2 MB**; **10** jobs/min/IP, **60** runs/min/IP; **2** concurrent sandbox builds (`TERMINAL_MAX_CONCURRENT_BUILDS`), **4** concurrent emulations (`TERMINAL_MAX_CONCURRENT_RUNS`); emulation cap **2^20 − 1** cycles and **60 s** wall clock; sandbox timeouts solc **30 s**, build-sbf **300 s**, rand-guest build **180 s**; stdout/stderr capture ≤ **1 MB** each; jobs expire **1 hour** after last access; disk cap **20 GB** (`TERMINAL_DATA_CAP_GB`).
- Trace files: `cycles.bin` 36-byte records, register checkpoint every **1 024** cycles, memory checkpoint every **65 536** cycles, trace pages ≤ **4 096** records, memory windows ≤ **256** words.
- All HTTP errors are JSON `{error, message, details?}`; stage failures are data on the job, not HTTP errors.
- Web: Next **15.5** App Router, React **19**, Tailwind **4**, `output: 'standalone'`, security headers from `next.config.mjs`, fonts self-hosted through `next/font`, dark ink theme by default from `clients/design/tokens.json` with cobalt accent `#5B8DEF`, light theme under `html[data-theme="light"]`; no state library; no Monaco.
- Tests that need Docker, the sandbox image, or a RISC-V clang skip with `eprintln!("skipping: TERMINAL_SANDBOX unset")` (or `TERMINAL_TOOLCHAIN`), never fail.
- Commit after every task with the attribution lines in the session reminder.

## Review Focus

1. **A Solidity file that defines several contracts and no `contract` option** — the job must fail with `unsupported: choose a contract` naming the deployable ones, not pick one silently. Test added to Task 12.
2. **An ABI argument the encoder cannot express (`string`, `bytes`, arrays, structs)** — the run must be refused with a message naming the type, not encoded wrongly. Test added to Task 10.
3. **A run that traps mid-way (out of cycles, bad pc, a call needing more than 16 storage witnesses)** — the run summary carries `trap` (and `trap_clk` for out-of-cycles); the emulator returns no partial trace, so the trace endpoints answer 409 saying so, and witness discovery stops at `MAX_WITNESSES` with a clear error. Tests added to Tasks 9, 10 and 18.
4. **An image bigger than the configured cap, or an ELF the loader refuses** — the image view says `fits: false` with the cap, and `rand-guest check` findings are shown; the run stage is still allowed (the emulator does not care about the cap). Test added to Task 7.
5. **Memory and register state requested at cycle 0, at the last cycle, and past the end** — cycle 0 is all-zero registers, the last cycle is the state *before* that cycle executes, and past-the-end is a 400. Tests added to Tasks 8 and 18.

## File structure

```
terminal/
  .gitignore  .gitmodules  README.md
  circuits/                                   submodule @ 9bf3844
  api/
    Cargo.toml  rust-toolchain.toml
    crates/terminal-core/
      Cargo.toml
      src/lib.rs
      src/views.rs            every serde DTO the API serves (the contract with web/)
      src/rv_disasm.rs        RV32IM text for `rand_zkvm::isa::Instr`
      src/sbpf_disasm.rs      sBPF text for `sbpf_core::isa::Insn`
      src/evm_analysis.rs     EVM compile view + transpile view from runtime bytecode
      src/sbpf_analysis.rs    sBPF compile view + transpile view from an ELF
      src/source_map.rs       C markers, `llvm-dwarfdump --debug-line` text, composition
      src/image.rs            image view from image.bin
      src/trace.rs            compact trace: write from `Execution`, read, replay, summary
      src/tiers.rs            per-tier capacities and placement
      src/evm_call.rs         EVM call form → `EvmCall`, ABI encoding, witness discovery
      src/sbpf_call.rs        Solana call form → `SbpfCall`
      src/stages.rs           filesystem side of each stage (solc json in/out, shim writing, build outputs)
      tests/fixtures/         erc20.runtime.hex, erc20.image.bin, erc20.contract.c, erc20.info.txt,
                              erc20-transfer.words, spl_token.so, spl.image.bin, spl.program.c, spl.info.txt,
                              spl-transfer.public, spl-transfer.input, solc-output.json, debug-line.txt, symbols.txt
      tests/*.rs
    crates/terminal-sandbox/
      Cargo.toml  src/lib.rs  (Runner trait, DockerRunner, FakeRunner)
    crates/terminal-api/
      Cargo.toml
      src/main.rs  src/lib.rs (config)  src/error.rs  src/state.rs  src/ratelimit.rs
      src/jobs.rs (store + pipeline driver + events)  src/routes.rs
      src/handlers/{mod.rs, jobs.rs, runs.rs, samples.rs}
      tests/common/mod.rs  tests/jobs.rs  tests/runs.rs  tests/sandbox.rs (gated)
  sandbox/
    Dockerfile  entrypoints/{solc.sh, build-sbf.sh, rand-guest-build.sh}  solana-template/{Cargo.toml, src/lib.rs}
  samples/
    erc20/{ERC20.sol, meta.json}  counter/{Counter.sol, meta.json}
    spl-token/{spl_token.so, meta.json}  solana-counter/{Cargo.toml, src/lib.rs, meta.json}
  web/
    package.json  next.config.mjs  tsconfig.json  postcss.config.mjs  vitest.config.ts  playwright.config.ts
    app/{layout.tsx, page.tsx, globals.css, fonts/}
    lib/{api.ts, types.ts, format.ts, replay.ts, graph-layout.ts}
    components/{Shell.tsx, PipelineStrip.tsx, Editor.tsx, SamplesPanel.tsx, Console.tsx,
                CompilePanel.tsx, AnalysePanel.tsx, TranspilePanel.tsx, BlockGraph.tsx, CViewer.tsx,
                ImagePanel.tsx, RunPanel.tsx, EvmCallForm.tsx, SbpfCallForm.tsx,
                Debugger.tsx, Timeline.tsx, Listing.tsx, Registers.tsx, MemoryPanel.tsx, SyscallLog.tsx, TablesPanel.tsx}
    test/*.test.ts  e2e/{mock-api.mjs, smoke.spec.ts, live.spec.ts}
  deploy/
    deploy.sh  nginx.conf  terminal-web.service  terminal-api.service  cloud-init.yaml
  .github/workflows/{ci.yml, coverage.yml}
  docs/superpowers/{specs,plans}/
```

Job directory (`$TERMINAL_DATA/jobs/<id>/`):

```
job.json                       JobRecord (lang, options, stage statuses, console)
source/<files>                 the submitted files
compile/  runtime.hex creation.hex abi.json | program.so   view.json     (host-owned, regular files)
  sandbox/  the only mount of the compile step: solc: input.json -> output.json;
            build-sbf: src/lib.rs src/Cargo.toml -> program.so (+ the tool's own scratch)
transpile/ contract.c|program.c view.json
build/    image.bin image-dbg.bin debug-line.txt source_map.json   view.json     (host-owned, regular files)
  sandbox/  the only mount of the build step: shim/ (the generated crate)  shim-dbg/ (the -g copy)
            -> image.bin image-dbg.bin debug-line.txt (taken back, size-capped, no links followed)
transpile/ also op_map.json
$TERMINAL_DATA/runs/<rid>/  run.json (job id, lang) cycles.bin regwrites.bin memwrites.bin regs.ckpt memckpt.bin memckpt.idx syscalls.json summary.json
```

In the sandbox the build directory is bind-mounted at `/circuits/jobs/job`, so the shim crate sits at `/circuits/jobs/job/shim` and its relative checkout root is `../../..`.

Add `api/crates/terminal-core/tests/fixtures.rs` (a shared helper, included by other test files with `mod fixtures;` via `#[path]`):

```rust
//! Paths and loaders for the checked-in fixtures. Included by every test file with
//! `#[path = "fixtures.rs"] mod fixtures;`.
#![allow(dead_code)]
use std::path::PathBuf;

pub fn dir() -> PathBuf { PathBuf::from(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")).join("tests/fixtures") }
pub fn bytes(name: &str) -> Vec<u8> { std::fs::read(dir().join(name)).unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("{name}: {e}")) }
pub fn text(name: &str) -> String { String::from_utf8(bytes(name)).unwrap() }
/// `erc20.runtime.hex` as bytes (the file is hex text with a trailing newline).
pub fn erc20_code() -> Vec<u8> { hex::decode(text("erc20.runtime.hex").trim().trim_start_matches("0x")).unwrap() }
/// Whitespace-separated decimal words, as `rand-guest run --input` takes them.
pub fn words(name: &str) -> Vec<u32> { text(name).split_whitespace().map(|w| w.parse().unwrap()).collect() }
```

Since the crate has no tests yet, add `api/crates/terminal-core/tests/smoke.rs`:

```rust
    assert_eq!(fixtures::bytes("spl.image.bin").len(), 260_408);
}
```

- [ ] **Step 5: README and commit**

`README.md`:
```markdown
# Rand Terminal

randterminal.org: a browser IDE for Ethereum and Solana developers. Paste Solidity or a Solana
program, watch it become an RV32IM image through `evm2rv` / `sbpf2rv`, and step through its run
on the Rand zkVM.

- `api/` — Rust service (axum). `cd api && cargo test`.
- `web/` — Next.js app. `cd web && npm ci && npm run dev`.
- `sandbox/` — the container image the untrusted tool steps run in.
- `circuits/` — pinned submodule supplying the transpilers and the zkVM.

Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-terminal-design.md`.
```

```bash
git add -A && git commit -m "scaffold: api workspace, circuits submodule, fixtures"
```

**Files:**
- Create: `api/crates/terminal-core/src/views.rs`, `api/crates/terminal-core/src/rv_disasm.rs`
- Modify: `api/crates/terminal-core/src/lib.rs`
- Test: `api/crates/terminal-core/tests/rv_disasm.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Produces: every struct in `views.rs` below (later tasks fill them); `rv_disasm::text(instr: &Instr) -> String`, `rv_disasm::reg_name(r: u32) -> &'static str`, `rv_disasm::ecall_name(a7: u32) -> Option<&'static str>`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing disassembler test**

`api/crates/terminal-core/tests/rv_disasm.rs`:
```rust
use rand_zkvm::isa::{AluOp, BranchCond, Instr, Width};
use terminal_core::rv_disasm::{ecall_name, reg_name, text};

#[test]
fn abi_register_names() {
    assert_eq!(reg_name(0), "zero");
    assert_eq!(reg_name(1), "ra");
    assert_eq!(reg_name(2), "sp");
    assert_eq!(reg_name(8), "s0");
    assert_eq!(reg_name(10), "a0");
    assert_eq!(reg_name(17), "a7");
    assert_eq!(reg_name(31), "t6");
}

#[test]
fn formats_each_shape() {
    assert_eq!(text(&Instr::Lui { rd: 10, imm: 0x12345000 }), "lui a0, 0x12345");
    assert_eq!(text(&Instr::Auipc { rd: 2, imm: 0x1000 }), "auipc sp, 0x1");
    assert_eq!(text(&Instr::Jal { rd: 1, imm: 8 }), "jal ra, 8");
    assert_eq!(text(&Instr::Jal { rd: 0, imm: (-4i32) as u32 }), "j -4");
    assert_eq!(text(&Instr::Jalr { rd: 0, rs1: 1, imm: 0 }), "ret");
    assert_eq!(text(&Instr::Jalr { rd: 1, rs1: 5, imm: 12 }), "jalr ra, 12(t0)");
    assert_eq!(text(&Instr::Branch { cond: BranchCond::Eq, rs1: 10, rs2: 0, imm: 16 }), "beq a0, zero, 16");
    assert_eq!(text(&Instr::Branch { cond: BranchCond::Geu, rs1: 11, rs2: 12, imm: (-8i32) as u32 }), "bgeu a1, a2, -8");
    assert_eq!(text(&Instr::Load { rd: 10, rs1: 2, imm: 4, width: Width::Word, signed: true }), "lw a0, 4(sp)");
    assert_eq!(text(&Instr::Load { rd: 10, rs1: 2, imm: 0, width: Width::Byte, signed: false }), "lbu a0, 0(sp)");
    assert_eq!(text(&Instr::Load { rd: 10, rs1: 2, imm: 0, width: Width::Half, signed: true }), "lh a0, 0(sp)");
    assert_eq!(text(&Instr::Store { rs1: 2, rs2: 10, imm: (-4i32) as u32, width: Width::Word }), "sw a0, -4(sp)");
    assert_eq!(text(&Instr::Store { rs1: 2, rs2: 10, imm: 0, width: Width::Byte }), "sb a0, 0(sp)");
    assert_eq!(text(&Instr::AluImm { op: AluOp::Add, rd: 10, rs1: 0, imm: 7 }), "li a0, 7");
    assert_eq!(text(&Instr::AluImm { op: AluOp::Add, rd: 10, rs1: 10, imm: (-1i32) as u32 }), "addi a0, a0, -1");
    assert_eq!(text(&Instr::AluImm { op: AluOp::Sll, rd: 10, rs1: 10, imm: 2 }), "slli a0, a0, 2");
    assert_eq!(text(&Instr::AluImm { op: AluOp::Sltu, rd: 10, rs1: 11, imm: 1 }), "seqz a0, a1");
    assert_eq!(text(&Instr::AluReg { op: AluOp::Mul, rd: 10, rs1: 11, rs2: 12 }), "mul a0, a1, a2");
    assert_eq!(text(&Instr::AluReg { op: AluOp::Sub, rd: 10, rs1: 0, rs2: 11 }), "neg a0, a1");
    assert_eq!(text(&Instr::AluReg { op: AluOp::Remu, rd: 10, rs1: 11, rs2: 12 }), "remu a0, a1, a2");
    assert_eq!(text(&Instr::Ecall), "ecall");
}

#[test]
fn ecall_names() {
    assert_eq!(ecall_name(0), Some("HALT"));
    assert_eq!(ecall_name(1), Some("WRITE_OUTPUT"));
    assert_eq!(ecall_name(2), Some("READ_INPUT"));
    assert_eq!(ecall_name(3), Some("POSEIDON2"));
    assert_eq!(ecall_name(4), Some("KECCAK"));
    assert_eq!(ecall_name(5), Some("SHA256"));
    assert_eq!(ecall_name(6), Some("READ_PUBLIC"));
    assert_eq!(ecall_name(7), Some("POSEIDON2_LEN"));
    assert_eq!(ecall_name(8), None);
}

#[test]
fn round_trips_through_encode_decode() {
    for i in [
        Instr::Lui { rd: 10, imm: 0x12345000 },
        Instr::Jalr { rd: 1, rs1: 5, imm: 12 },
        Instr::AluReg { op: AluOp::Mulhsu, rd: 3, rs1: 4, rs2: 5 },
        Instr::Branch { cond: BranchCond::Ltu, rs1: 1, rs2: 2, imm: 32 },
    ] {
        let back = Instr::decode(i.encode()).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(text(&back), text(&i));
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `rv_disasm.rs`**

```rust
//! RV32IM text for `rand_zkvm::isa::Instr`, GNU-assembler style with ABI register names and the
//! usual pseudo-instructions (`li`, `j`, `ret`, `neg`, `seqz`), which is what a developer reading
//! objdump output expects.
use rand_zkvm::isa::{AluOp, BranchCond, Instr, Width};

const NAMES: [&str; 32] = [
    "zero", "ra", "sp", "gp", "tp", "t0", "t1", "t2", "s0", "s1", "a0", "a1", "a2", "a3", "a4", "a5",
    "a6", "a7", "s2", "s3", "s4", "s5", "s6", "s7", "s8", "s9", "s10", "s11", "t3", "t4", "t5", "t6",
];

pub fn reg_name(r: u32) -> &'static str { NAMES[(r as usize) & 31] }

pub fn ecall_name(a7: u32) -> Option<&'static str> {
    Some(match a7 {
        0 => "HALT", 1 => "WRITE_OUTPUT", 2 => "READ_INPUT", 3 => "POSEIDON2", 4 => "KECCAK",
        5 => "SHA256", 6 => "READ_PUBLIC", 7 => "POSEIDON2_LEN", _ => return None,
    })
}

fn alu_name(op: AluOp) -> &'static str {
    match op {
        AluOp::Add => "add", AluOp::Sub => "sub", AluOp::And => "and", AluOp::Or => "or", AluOp::Xor => "xor",
        AluOp::Sll => "sll", AluOp::Srl => "srl", AluOp::Sra => "sra", AluOp::Slt => "slt", AluOp::Sltu => "sltu",
        AluOp::Eq => "eq", AluOp::Mul => "mul", AluOp::Mulh => "mulh", AluOp::Mulhu => "mulhu",
        AluOp::Mulhsu => "mulhsu", AluOp::Div => "div", AluOp::Divu => "divu", AluOp::Rem => "rem", AluOp::Remu => "remu",
    }
}

fn branch_name(c: BranchCond) -> &'static str {
    match c { BranchCond::Eq => "beq", BranchCond::Ne => "bne", BranchCond::Lt => "blt", BranchCond::Ge => "bge", BranchCond::Ltu => "bltu", BranchCond::Geu => "bgeu" }
}

fn simm(imm: u32) -> i32 { imm as i32 }

fn width_suffix(w: Width, signed: bool, load: bool) -> &'static str {
    match (w, signed || !load) {
        (Width::Byte, true) => "b", (Width::Byte, false) => "bu",
        (Width::Half, true) => "h", (Width::Half, false) => "hu",
        (Width::Word, _) => "w",
    }
}

pub fn text(i: &Instr) -> String {
    let r = reg_name;
    match *i {
        Instr::Lui { rd, imm } => format!("lui {}, {:#x}", r(rd), imm >> 12),
        Instr::Auipc { rd, imm } => format!("auipc {}, {:#x}", r(rd), imm >> 12),
        Instr::Jal { rd: 0, imm } => format!("j {}", simm(imm)),
        Instr::Jal { rd, imm } => format!("jal {}, {}", r(rd), simm(imm)),
        Instr::Jalr { rd: 0, rs1: 1, imm: 0 } => "ret".to_string(),
        Instr::Jalr { rd, rs1, imm } => format!("jalr {}, {}({})", r(rd), simm(imm), r(rs1)),
        Instr::Branch { cond, rs1, rs2, imm } => format!("{} {}, {}, {}", branch_name(cond), r(rs1), r(rs2), simm(imm)),
        Instr::Load { rd, rs1, imm, width, signed } => format!("l{} {}, {}({})", width_suffix(width, signed, true), r(rd), simm(imm), r(rs1)),
        Instr::Store { rs1, rs2, imm, width } => format!("s{} {}, {}({})", width_suffix(width, true, false), r(rs2), simm(imm), r(rs1)),
        Instr::AluImm { op: AluOp::Add, rd, rs1: 0, imm } => format!("li {}, {}", r(rd), simm(imm)),
        Instr::AluImm { op: AluOp::Sltu, rd, rs1, imm: 1 } => format!("seqz {}, {}", r(rd), r(rs1)),
        Instr::AluImm { op, rd, rs1, imm } => format!("{}i {}, {}, {}", alu_name(op), r(rd), r(rs1), simm(imm)),
        Instr::AluReg { op: AluOp::Sub, rd, rs1: 0, rs2 } => format!("neg {}, {}", r(rd), r(rs2)),
        Instr::AluReg { op, rd, rs1, rs2 } => format!("{} {}, {}, {}", alu_name(op), r(rd), r(rs1), r(rs2)),
        Instr::Ecall => "ecall".to_string(),
    }
}
```

Note the `Lui`/`Auipc` immediates: `Instr` stores the full 32-bit value (low 12 bits zero); GNU prints the upper 20 bits.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

`api/crates/terminal-core/tests/sbpf_disasm.rs`:
```rust
use sbpf_core::isa::{decode, Insn};
use terminal_core::sbpf_disasm::{syscall_name, text};

fn i(opc: u8, dst: u8, src: u8, off: i16, imm: i32) -> Insn { Insn { opc, dst, src, off, imm } }

#[test]
fn alu_and_mov() {
    assert_eq!(text(0, &i(0x07, 1, 0, 0, 16), None), "add64 r1, 16");
    assert_eq!(text(0, &i(0x0f, 1, 2, 0, 0), None), "add64 r1, r2");
    assert_eq!(text(0, &i(0x04, 3, 0, 0, -1), None), "add32 r3, -1");
    assert_eq!(text(0, &i(0xb7, 0, 0, 0, 0), None), "mov64 r0, 0");
    assert_eq!(text(0, &i(0xbf, 6, 1, 0, 0), None), "mov64 r6, r1");
    assert_eq!(text(0, &i(0x67, 1, 0, 0, 32), None), "lsh64 r1, 32");
    assert_eq!(text(0, &i(0xc7, 1, 0, 0, 3), None), "arsh64 r1, 3");
    assert_eq!(text(0, &i(0x87, 1, 0, 0, 0), None), "neg64 r1");
    assert_eq!(text(0, &i(0xd4, 1, 0, 0, 16), None), "le16 r1");
    assert_eq!(text(0, &i(0xdc, 1, 0, 0, 64), None), "be64 r1");
}

#[test]
fn loads_stores() {
    assert_eq!(text(0, &i(0x79, 1, 10, -8, 0), None), "ldxdw r1, [r10-8]");
    assert_eq!(text(0, &i(0x61, 1, 2, 4, 0), None), "ldxw r1, [r2+4]");
    assert_eq!(text(0, &i(0x71, 1, 2, 0, 0), None), "ldxb r1, [r2+0]");
    assert_eq!(text(0, &i(0x7b, 10, 1, -16, 0), None), "stxdw [r10-16], r1");
    assert_eq!(text(0, &i(0x72, 10, 0, -1, 7), None), "stb [r10-1], 7");
    let lo = i(0x18, 1, 0, 0, 0x5678);
    let hi = i(0x00, 0, 0, 0, 0x1234);
    assert_eq!(text(0, &lo, Some(&hi)), "lddw r1, 0x123400005678");
}

#[test]
fn jumps_calls_exit() {
    assert_eq!(text(10, &i(0x05, 0, 0, 5, 0), None), "ja +5 ; -> 16");
    assert_eq!(text(10, &i(0x15, 1, 0, -3, 0), None), "jeq r1, 0, -3 ; -> 8");
    assert_eq!(text(10, &i(0x1d, 1, 2, 2, 0), None), "jeq r1, r2, +2 ; -> 13");
    assert_eq!(text(10, &i(0xad, 1, 2, 1, 0), None), "jlt r1, r2, +1 ; -> 12");
    assert_eq!(text(10, &i(0x85, 0, 0, 0, 4), None), "call +4 ; -> 15");
    assert_eq!(text(10, &i(0x85, 0, 1, 0, 0x1234), None), "call 0x1234");
    assert_eq!(text(10, &i(0x85, 0, 0, 0, 0x207559bd_u32 as i32), None), "syscall sol_log_");
    assert_eq!(text(10, &i(0x8d, 0, 0, 0, 3), None), "callx r3");
    assert_eq!(text(10, &i(0x95, 0, 0, 0, 0), None), "exit");
    assert_eq!(text(10, &i(0xff, 0, 0, 0, 0), None), "db 0xff");
}

#[test]
fn syscall_names_match_the_transpiler() {
    for (hash, cname) in sbpf2rv::emit::SYSCALLS {
        let name = syscall_name(hash).expect(cname);
        assert_eq!(cname, &format!("sbpf_sys_{}", name.trim_matches('_')), "{name}");
    }
    assert_eq!(syscall_name(0xdeadbeef), None);
}

#[test]
fn decodes_real_slots() {
    // `exit` encodes as opc 0x95 and everything else zero.
    let slot = u64::from_le_bytes([0x95, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0]);
    assert_eq!(text(0, &decode(slot), None), "exit");
}
```

The `call` convention: in sBPF v1, `call imm` with `src == 0` is a syscall when `imm` matches a known hash, else a relative call (`pc + 1 + imm`); `src == 1` is a static call to an absolute target. The test fixes the printing of each.

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `sbpf_disasm.rs`**

```rust
//! sBPF (Solana BPF v1) text for `sbpf_core::isa::Insn`, in the mnemonics `solana-rbpf`'s
//! disassembler prints, so a developer can compare against `cargo build-sbf --dump`.
use sbpf_core::isa::{lddw_imm64, Insn};

/// The twelve syscalls the transpiler supports, by murmur3 hash, named as Solana names them.
/// Order and hashes come from `sbpf2rv::emit::SYSCALLS` (tested).
pub fn syscall_name(hash: u32) -> Option<&'static str> {
    const NAMES: [&str; 12] = ["abort", "sol_panic_", "sol_log_", "sol_log_64_", "sol_log_compute_units_", "sol_log_pubkey", "sol_memcpy_", "sol_memmove_", "sol_memset_", "sol_memcmp_", "sol_alloc_free_", "sol_sha256"];
    sbpf2rv::emit::SYSCALLS.iter().position(|(h, _)| *h == hash).map(|i| NAMES[i])
}

fn alu(op: u8) -> Option<&'static str> {
    Some(match op & 0xf0 {
        0x00 => "add", 0x10 => "sub", 0x20 => "mul", 0x30 => "div", 0x40 => "or", 0x50 => "and", 0x60 => "lsh",
        0x70 => "rsh", 0x80 => "neg", 0x90 => "mod", 0xa0 => "xor", 0xb0 => "mov", 0xc0 => "arsh", 0xd0 => "end",
        _ => return None,
    })
}

fn jmp(op: u8) -> Option<&'static str> {
    Some(match op & 0xf0 {
        0x00 => "ja", 0x10 => "jeq", 0x20 => "jgt", 0x30 => "jge", 0x40 => "jset", 0x50 => "jne", 0x60 => "jsgt",
        0x70 => "jsge", 0xa0 => "jlt", 0xb0 => "jle", 0xc0 => "jslt", 0xd0 => "jsle",
        _ => return None,
    })
}

fn size(op: u8) -> &'static str { match op & 0x18 { 0x00 => "w", 0x08 => "h", 0x10 => "b", _ => "dw" } }

fn mem(reg: u8, off: i16) -> String { if off < 0 { format!("[r{reg}{off}]") } else { format!("[r{reg}+{off}]") } }

fn rel(pc: usize, off: i64) -> String { let t = pc as i64 + 1 + off; if off < 0 { format!("{off} ; -> {t}") } else { format!("+{off} ; -> {t}") } }

pub fn text(pc: usize, i: &Insn, next: Option<&Insn>) -> String {
    let (opc, dst, src, off, imm) = (i.opc, i.dst, i.src, i.off, i.imm);
    match opc & 0x07 {
        0x07 | 0x04 => {
            let w = if opc & 0x07 == 0x07 { "64" } else { "32" };
            let Some(name) = alu(opc) else { return format!("db {opc:#04x}") };
            match name {
                "neg" => format!("neg{w} r{dst}"),
                "end" => format!("{}{} r{dst}", if opc & 0x08 != 0 { "be" } else { "le" }, imm),
                _ if opc & 0x08 != 0 => format!("{name}{w} r{dst}, r{src}"),
                _ => format!("{name}{w} r{dst}, {imm}"),
            }
        }
        0x05 => match opc {
            0x85 if src == 0 && syscall_name(imm as u32).is_some() => format!("syscall {}", syscall_name(imm as u32).unwrap()),
            0x85 if src == 0 => format!("call {}", rel(pc, imm as i64)),
            0x85 => format!("call {:#x}", imm),
            0x8d => format!("callx r{imm}"),
            0x95 => "exit".to_string(),
            0x05 => format!("ja {}", rel(pc, off as i64)),
            _ => match jmp(opc) {
                Some(name) if opc & 0x08 != 0 => format!("{name} r{dst}, r{src}, {}", rel(pc, off as i64)),
                Some(name) => format!("{name} r{dst}, {imm}, {}", rel(pc, off as i64)),
                None => format!("db {opc:#04x}"),
            },
        },
        0x00 if opc == 0x18 => match next {
            Some(hi) => format!("lddw r{dst}, {:#x}", lddw_imm64(*i, *hi)),
            None => format!("lddw r{dst}, {:#x} ; truncated", imm as u32),
        },
        0x01 => format!("ldx{} r{dst}, {}", size(opc), mem(src, off)),
        0x02 => format!("st{} {}, {imm}", size(opc), mem(dst, off)),
        0x03 => format!("stx{} {}, r{src}", size(opc), mem(dst, off)),
        _ => format!("db {opc:#04x}"),
    }
}
```

```rust
#[path = "fixtures.rs"]
mod fixtures;
use terminal_core::evm_analysis::{compile_view, transpile, EvmTranspileError};
use terminal_core::source_map::evm_markers;
use terminal_core::views::OpLine;

#[test]
fn erc20_compile_view_counts() {
    let code = fixtures::erc20_code();
    let v = compile_view(&code, "ERC20", vec!["ERC20".into()], String::new(), serde_json::json!([]));
    assert_eq!(v.size_bytes, 1296);
    assert_eq!(v.runtime_hex, hex::encode(&code));
    assert_eq!(v.blocks, 74);
    assert_eq!(v.jumpdests, 51);
    assert_eq!(v.opcodes.len(), 798);
    let first = &v.opcodes[0];
    assert_eq!((first.pc, first.opcode, first.mnemonic.as_str(), first.push_hex.as_deref()), (0, 0x60, "PUSH1", Some("80")));
    assert_eq!(first.static_gas, 3);
    assert!(!first.jumpdest);
    assert!(v.opcodes.iter().filter(|o| o.jumpdest).count() == 51);
    // blocks are numbered in order and every opcode belongs to the block that contains its pc
    assert!(v.opcodes.windows(2).all(|w| w[0].block <= w[1].block));
}

#[test]
fn erc20_transpiles_at_stage_two() {
    let code = fixtures::erc20_code();
    let (view, c) = transpile(&code, 13, 2).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(view.stats.blocks, 74);
    assert_eq!(view.stats.ops, 798);
    assert!(view.stats.locals > 0);
    assert_eq!(view.stats.c_lines, c.lines().count());
    assert!(c.contains("/* 0x0000 PUSH1 0x80 */"));
    assert_eq!(view.graph.nodes.len(), 74);
    assert_eq!(view.graph.nodes[0].start, 0);
    // every edge points at an existing node
    assert!(view.graph.edges.iter().all(|e| e.to < 74 && e.from < 74));
    // the op_map is the markers of the C that was returned
    assert_eq!(view.op_map, evm_markers(&c));
    assert_eq!(view.op_map[0], OpLine { pc: 0, line: c.lines().position(|l| l.trim_start().starts_with("/* 0x0000 ")).unwrap() + 1 });
    assert!(view.op_map.len() >= 700);
    // the ERC-20 has no trapping opcodes
    assert!(view.warnings.iter().all(|w| !w.fatal));
}

    let code = vec![0x42, 0x00];

    let w = view.warnings.iter().find(|w| w.kind == "trap").expect("a trap warning");
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the marker parsers in `source_map.rs`**

```rust
//! Source maps: the C markers the transpilers emit, the DWARF line table of the `-g` side build,
//! the symbol table of the production ELF, and their composition. Task 6 adds the DWARF and nm
//! parsers and `compose`.
use crate::views::OpLine;

/// evm2rv writes `/* 0xPC MNEMONIC ... */` at the start (after indentation) of the C for each
/// opcode. One entry per marker, in file order, lines 1-based.
pub fn evm_markers(c: &str) -> Vec<OpLine> {
    c.lines().enumerate().filter_map(|(i, l)| {
        let t = l.trim_start();
        let rest = t.strip_prefix("/* 0x")?;
        let hex: String = rest.chars().take_while(|ch| ch.is_ascii_hexdigit()).collect();
        if hex.is_empty() || !rest[hex.len()..].starts_with(' ') { return None; }
        Some(OpLine { pc: usize::from_str_radix(&hex, 16).ok()?, line: i + 1 })
    }).collect()
}

/// sbpf2rv labels every block `L_<pc>:` (pc in slots). One entry per label, lines 1-based.
pub fn sbpf_labels(c: &str) -> Vec<OpLine> {
    c.lines().enumerate().filter_map(|(i, l)| {
        let t = l.trim();
        let rest = t.strip_prefix("L_")?.strip_suffix(':')?;
        Some(OpLine { pc: rest.parse().ok()?, line: i + 1 })
    }).collect()
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write `evm_analysis.rs`**

```rust
//! The EVM side of stages 1 and 2: what the bytecode looks like to the transpiler, and what the
//! transpiler makes of it.
use crate::source_map::evm_markers;
use crate::views::*;
use evm2rv::blocks::{self, Block, Term, Warning};
use evm2rv::emit::{self, EmitError, Options, Stage};

#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
pub enum EvmTranspileError {
    #[error("the code uses CHAINID at pc {pc}; a chain id is required")]
    ChainIdRequired { pc: usize },
    #[error("stage must be 1 or 2, not {0}")]
    BadStage(u8),
    #[error("unsupported: {}", .0.detail)]
    Unsupported(WarningView),
}

fn push_hex(op: &blocks::Op) -> Option<String> {
    let bytes = op.push?;
    let n = (op.opcode as usize).checked_sub(0x5f)?.clamp(1, 32);
    Some(hex::encode(&bytes[32 - n..]))
}

pub fn compile_view(code: &[u8], contract: &str, contracts: Vec<String>, creation_hex: String, abi: serde_json::Value) -> EvmCompileView {
    let bs = blocks::blocks(code);
    let jd = blocks::jumpdests(code);
    let mut opcodes = Vec::new();
    for (bi, b) in bs.iter().enumerate() {
        for op in &b.ops {
            opcodes.push(EvmOpcode {
                pc: op.pc, opcode: op.opcode, mnemonic: emit::mnemonic(op.opcode), push_hex: push_hex(op),
                static_gas: blocks::static_gas(op.opcode), jumpdest: op.opcode == 0x5b, block: bi,
            });
        }
    }
    EvmCompileView {
        contract: contract.to_string(), contracts, creation_hex, runtime_hex: hex::encode(code), size_bytes: code.len(),
        abi, opcodes, blocks: bs.len(), jumpdests: jd.len(),
    }
}

fn term_text(t: &Term) -> String {
    match t {
        Term::Fallthrough(pc) => format!("fallthrough → {pc:#06x}"),
        Term::Jump => "jump (dynamic)".into(),
        Term::JumpI(pc) => format!("jumpi, else → {pc:#06x}"),
        Term::Stop => "stop".into(), Term::Return => "return".into(), Term::Revert => "revert".into(),
        Term::Invalid => "invalid".into(), Term::TrapOp(op) => format!("trap {}", emit::mnemonic(*op)),
        Term::OutOfBounds => "code too large".into(),
    }
}

pub fn graph(bs: &[Block]) -> Graph {
    let index_of = |pc: usize| bs.iter().position(|b| b.start == pc);
    let nodes = bs.iter().enumerate().map(|(i, b)| GraphNode {
        id: i, label: format!("{:#06x}", b.start), start: b.start, end: b.end, term: term_text(&b.term), function: None, static_gas: Some(b.static_gas),
    }).collect();
    let mut edges = Vec::new();
    for (i, b) in bs.iter().enumerate() {
        match b.term {
            Term::Fallthrough(pc) => if let Some(to) = index_of(pc) { edges.push(GraphEdge { from: i, to, kind: "fallthrough".into() }) },
            Term::JumpI(pc) => if let Some(to) = index_of(pc) { edges.push(GraphEdge { from: i, to, kind: "not-taken".into() }) },
            _ => {}
        }
    }
    Graph { nodes, edges }
}

pub fn warning_view(w: &Warning) -> WarningView {
    match *w {
        Warning::Trap { pc, opcode } => WarningView {
            kind: "trap".into(), pc: Some(pc), detail: format!("{} at {pc:#06x}", emit::mnemonic(opcode)),
            explanation: "Block-context, balance, external-code and CREATE opcodes have no meaning inside a proof: the translated program halts with a trap if this opcode executes. Code that never reaches it runs normally.".into(),
            fatal: false,
        },
        Warning::Call { pc, opcode } => WarningView {
            kind: "call".into(), pc: Some(pc), detail: format!("{} at {pc:#06x}", emit::mnemonic(opcode)),
            explanation: "Calls into other contracts are translated as calls into the precompile set only; a CALL with a nonzero value or to an unknown address traps at run time.".into(),
            fatal: false,
        },
        Warning::CodeTooLarge { len } => WarningView {
            kind: "code_too_large".into(), pc: None, detail: format!("{len} bytes of runtime code; the limit is {}", evm_core::interp::MAX_CODE_BYTES),
            explanation: "The guest's code buffer is fixed at MAX_CODE_BYTES; split the contract or strip metadata.".into(),
            fatal: true,
        },
    }
}

pub fn transpile(code: &[u8], chain_id: u64, stage: u8) -> Result<(TranspileView, String), EvmTranspileError> {
    let stage = match stage { 1 => Stage::One, 2 => Stage::Two, s => return Err(EvmTranspileError::BadStage(s)) };
    let warnings: Vec<WarningView> = blocks::warnings(code).iter().map(warning_view).collect();
    if let Some(f) = warnings.iter().find(|w| w.fatal) { return Err(EvmTranspileError::Unsupported(f.clone())); }
    let opts = Options { chain_id: Some(chain_id), stage };
    let emitted = emit::translate(code, &opts).map_err(|e| match e { EmitError::ChainIdRequired { pc } => EvmTranspileError::ChainIdRequired { pc } })?;
    let bs = blocks::blocks(code);
    let op_map = evm_markers(&emitted.c);
    let view = TranspileView {
        warnings, graph: graph(&bs), c_source: emitted.c.clone(), op_map,
        stats: TranspileStats { blocks: emitted.blocks, ops: emitted.opcodes, locals: emitted.locals, c_lines: emitted.c.lines().count(), functions: None },
    };
    Ok((view, emitted.c))
}
```

`chain_id: 0` in the test means "the user gave none": the API layer passes `Some(chain_id)` always (default 20), so `ChainIdRequired` can only surface if the caller passes `0`. Make that explicit: in `transpile`, use `chain_id: if chain_id == 0 { None } else { Some(chain_id) }`.

```rust
#[path = "fixtures.rs"]
mod fixtures;
use terminal_core::sbpf_analysis::{analyse, SbpfError};
use terminal_core::source_map::sbpf_labels;

#[test]
fn spl_token_analyses() {
    let elf = fixtures::bytes("spl_token.so");
    let a = analyse(&elf).unwrap();
    let c = &a.compile;
    assert_eq!(c.elf_bytes, elf.len());
    assert!(c.text_bytes > 100_000 && c.text_bytes % 8 == 0);
    assert!(c.functions.len() > 50, "{}", c.functions.len());
    assert!(c.instructions > 10_000);
    assert!(c.syscalls.iter().all(|s| s.supported), "SPL Token uses only supported syscalls: {:?}", c.syscalls);
    assert!(c.syscalls.iter().any(|s| s.name == "sol_log_"));
    assert!(c.warnings.iter().all(|w| !w.fatal));
    // the entry function comes first and starts at the ELF's entry pc
    assert_eq!(c.functions[0].entry, c.entry_pc);
    assert_eq!(c.functions[0].blocks[0].start, c.entry_pc);
    // every instruction has text and the first block's first pc is its start
    for f in &c.functions { for b in &f.blocks { assert_eq!(b.insns[0].pc, b.start); assert!(b.insns.iter().all(|i| !i.text.is_empty())); } }

    let t = &a.transpile;
    assert_eq!(t.stats.functions, Some(c.functions.len()));
    assert_eq!(t.graph.nodes.len(), c.functions.iter().map(|f| f.blocks.len()).sum::<usize>());
    assert!(t.graph.edges.iter().all(|e| e.to < t.graph.nodes.len()));
    assert!(t.graph.nodes.iter().all(|n| n.function.is_some()));
    assert_eq!(t.op_map, sbpf_labels(&t.c_source));
    assert!(t.op_map.len() >= 1000);
    assert!(a.program_c.ends_with(&sbpf2rv::shim::view_digest_c(&a.digest)));
    assert!(t.c_source.contains("sbpf_entry"));
}

#[test]
fn garbage_is_a_bad_elf() {
    match analyse(&[0u8; 64]) {
        Err(SbpfError::BadElf(_)) => {}
        other => panic!("{other:?}"),
    }
}

#[test]
fn labels_parse() {
    let c = "void f(void) {\nL_225:\n  x();\n    L_227:\n}\n";
    let l = sbpf_labels(c);
    assert_eq!(l.len(), 2);
    assert_eq!((l[0].pc, l[0].line), (225, 2));
    assert_eq!((l[1].pc, l[1].line), (227, 4));
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `sbpf_analysis.rs`**

```rust
//! The Solana side of stages 1 and 2: the ELF as the transpiler scans it, and the C it emits.
use crate::sbpf_disasm::{syscall_name, text};
use crate::source_map::sbpf_labels;
use crate::views::*;
use sbpf2rv::scan::{self, Scan, Term, Warning};
use sbpf2rv::{emit, shim};
use sbpf_core::elf::Program;
use std::collections::BTreeMap;

#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
pub enum SbpfError {
    #[error("not a loadable sBPF ELF: {0}")]
    BadElf(String),
    #[error("the program is too large to scan: {0}")]
    TooMuchWork(String),
    #[error("unsupported: {}", .0.detail)]
    Unsupported(WarningView),
}

pub struct Analysis { pub compile: SbpfCompileView, pub transpile: TranspileView, pub program_c: String, pub digest: [u32; 8] }

fn term_text(t: &Term) -> String {
    match t {
        Term::Fallthrough(n) => format!("fallthrough → {n}"),
        Term::Jump(n) => format!("ja → {n}"),
        Term::CondJump { taken, not } => format!("branch → {taken} / {not}"),
        Term::Exit => "exit".into(),
        Term::Call { target, next } => format!("call {target}, then {next}"),
        Term::Syscall { hash, next } => format!("syscall {}, then {next}", syscall_name(*hash).unwrap_or("?")),
        Term::CallX { next } => format!("callx, then {next}"),
        Term::Trap(k) => format!("trap {k:?}"),
    }
}

pub fn warning_view(w: &Warning) -> WarningView {
    let (kind, pc, detail, explanation, fatal) = match *w {
        Warning::UnknownSyscall { pc, hash } => ("unknown_syscall", pc, format!("syscall {hash:#010x} at {pc}"), "Only twelve Solana syscalls exist in the guest runtime; this one traps if executed.", false),
        Warning::Cpi { pc, name } => ("cpi", pc, format!("{name} at {pc}"), "Cross-program invocation is not available inside a proof; the program traps if it reaches this call.", false),
        Warning::RegisterOutOfRange { pc, opc } => ("register_out_of_range", pc, format!("opcode {opc:#04x} at {pc}"), "The instruction names a register above r10; the verifier would reject it and the guest traps.", false),
        Warning::UnknownOpcode { pc, opc } => ("unknown_opcode", pc, format!("opcode {opc:#04x} at {pc}"), "Not an sBPF v1 instruction; it traps if executed.", false),
        Warning::JumpOutOfText { pc, target } => ("jump_out_of_text", pc, format!("target {target} from {pc}"), "A jump past the text section traps.", false),
        Warning::BadCallImmSrc { pc, src } => ("bad_call", pc, format!("call with src {src} at {pc}"), "The call form is not one the translator knows; it traps.", false),
    };
    WarningView { kind: kind.into(), pc: Some(pc), detail, explanation: explanation.into(), fatal }
}

fn insn_views(p: &Program<'_>, b: &scan::Block) -> Vec<SbpfInsnView> {
    let _ = p;
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    let mut k = 0;
    while k < b.insns.len() {
        let i = &b.insns[k];
        let pc = b.start + k;
        let next = if i.opc == 0x18 { b.insns.get(k + 1) } else { None };
        out.push(SbpfInsnView { pc, text: text(pc, i, next), opc: i.opc });
        k += if i.opc == 0x18 { 2 } else { 1 };
    }
    out
}

fn compile_view(elf_len: usize, p: &Program<'_>, s: &Scan) -> SbpfCompileView {
    let mut sys: BTreeMap<u32, usize> = BTreeMap::new();
    let mut instructions = 0;
    let functions = s.functions.iter().map(|f| SbpfFunctionView {
        entry: f.entry,
        blocks: f.blocks.iter().map(|b| {
            if let Term::Syscall { hash, .. } = b.term { *sys.entry(hash).or_default() += 1; }
            instructions += b.insns.len();
            SbpfBlockView { start: b.start, end: b.end, term: term_text(&b.term), insns: insn_views(p, b) }
        }).collect(),
    }).collect();
    SbpfCompileView {
        elf_bytes: elf_len, text_bytes: p.text.len(), rodata_bytes: p.rodata.len(), entry_pc: p.entry_pc,
        functions, instructions,
        syscalls: sys.into_iter().map(|(hash, count)| SbpfSyscallView { hash, name: syscall_name(hash).map(str::to_string).unwrap_or_else(|| format!("unknown {hash:#010x}")), count, supported: syscall_name(hash).is_some() }).collect(),
        warnings: s.warnings.iter().map(warning_view).collect(),
    }
}

fn graph(s: &Scan) -> Graph {
    let mut nodes = Vec::new();
    let mut id_of: BTreeMap<usize, usize> = BTreeMap::new(); // block start → node id
    for (fi, f) in s.functions.iter().enumerate() {
        for b in &f.blocks {
            id_of.insert(b.start, nodes.len());
            nodes.push(GraphNode { id: nodes.len(), label: format!("{}", b.start), start: b.start, end: b.end, term: term_text(&b.term), function: Some(fi), static_gas: None });
        }
    }
    let mut edges = Vec::new();
    for f in &s.functions {
        for b in &f.blocks {
            let from = id_of[&b.start];
            let mut e = |to: usize, kind: &str| if let Some(&t) = id_of.get(&to) { edges.push(GraphEdge { from, to: t, kind: kind.into() }) };
            match b.term {
                Term::Fallthrough(n) => e(n, "fallthrough"),
                Term::Jump(n) => e(n, "jump"),
                Term::CondJump { taken, not } => { e(taken, "taken"); e(not, "not-taken"); }
                Term::Call { target, next } => { e(target, "call"); e(next, "return"); }
                Term::Syscall { next, .. } | Term::CallX { next } => e(next, "fallthrough"),
                Term::Exit | Term::Trap(_) => {}
            }
        }
    }
    Graph { nodes, edges }
}

pub fn analyse(elf: &[u8]) -> Result<Analysis, SbpfError> {
    let mut bytes = elf.to_vec();
    let p = sbpf_core::elf::load(&mut bytes).map_err(|h| SbpfError::BadElf(format!("{h:?}")))?;
    let s = scan::try_scan(&p).map_err(|e| SbpfError::TooMuchWork(e.to_string()))?;
    let compile = compile_view(elf.len(), &p, &s);
    let emitted = emit::emit_program(&p, &s);
    let digest = shim::view_digest(&p);
    let program_c = format!("{}{}", emitted.c, shim::view_digest_c(&digest));
    let op_map = sbpf_labels(&program_c);
    let transpile = TranspileView {
        warnings: compile.warnings.clone(), graph: graph(&s), c_source: program_c.clone(), op_map,
        stats: TranspileStats { blocks: emitted.blocks, ops: emitted.instructions, locals: 0, c_lines: program_c.lines().count(), functions: Some(emitted.functions) },
    };
    Ok(Analysis { compile, transpile, program_c, digest })
}
```

Check `sbpf2rv::shim::write_crate` to confirm it writes `program_c` followed by `view_digest_c(digest)` with no separator; if it inserts a newline, mirror it in `program_c` above so the shim file written in Task 13 equals what the CLI writes.

Add `pub mod sbpf_analysis;` to `lib.rs`.

Append to `source_map.rs`:
```rust
use crate::views::SourceMap;

/// Rows of `llvm-dwarfdump --debug-line` whose file is `c_file` (by base name). Only `is_stmt`
/// rows count (the others are mid-statement), `end_sequence` rows are not instructions, and the
/// first row wins when an address repeats.
pub fn parse_debug_line(text: &str, c_file: &str) -> Vec<(u32, usize)> {
    let mut files: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
    let mut pending_index: Option<usize> = None;
    let mut rows: Vec<(u32, usize)> = Vec::new();
    for line in text.lines() {
        let t = line.trim();
        if let Some(rest) = t.strip_prefix("file_names[") {
            pending_index = rest.split(']').next().and_then(|n| n.trim().parse().ok());
            continue;
        }
        if let (Some(i), Some(rest)) = (pending_index, t.strip_prefix("name: \"")) {
            let name = rest.trim_end_matches('"').to_string();
            if files.len() <= i { files.resize(i + 1, String::new()); }
            files[i] = name;
            pending_index = None;
            continue;
        }
        if !t.starts_with("0x") { continue; }
        let cols: Vec<&str> = t.split_whitespace().collect();
        if cols.len() < 4 { continue; }
        let Ok(addr) = u64::from_str_radix(cols[0].trim_start_matches("0x"), 16) else { continue };
        let Ok(ln) = cols[1].parse::<usize>() else { continue };
        let Ok(fi) = cols[3].parse::<usize>() else { continue };
        let flags = &cols[7..];
        if !flags.contains(&"is_stmt") || flags.contains(&"end_sequence") { continue; }
        let matches = files.get(fi).map(|f| f.rsplit('/').next() == Some(c_file)).unwrap_or(false);
        if !matches { continue; }
        rows.push((addr as u32, ln));
    }
    rows.sort_by_key(|r| r.0);
    rows.dedup_by_key(|r| r.0);
    rows
}

pub fn compose(pc_rows: &[(u32, usize)], op_map: &[OpLine]) -> SourceMap {
    let mut c_to_src: Vec<(usize, usize)> = op_map.iter().map(|o| (o.line, o.pc)).collect();
    c_to_src.sort();
    SourceMap { pc_to_c: pc_rows.to_vec(), c_to_src }
}

/// The source pc whose C begins at or before `line` (markers cover the lines after them).
pub fn src_pc_for_line(op_map: &[OpLine], line: usize) -> Option<usize> {
    op_map.iter().take_while(|o| o.line <= line).last().map(|o| o.pc)
}
```

The `files[fi]` index: DWARF 5 file indices are 0-based and `file_names[0]` is the primary file, matching the dump above; DWARF 4 numbers from 1 and the dump prints `file_names[  1]` first, which this parser also handles because it indexes by the printed number.

```rust
//! Stage 4's view: the image as the chain sees it (words, hc, program id, cap) and as a developer
//! reads it (RV32IM listing with the source map).
use crate::rv_disasm::{ecall_name, text};
use crate::source_map::{compose, parse_debug_line};
use crate::views::*;
use rand_guest::{chain, check, pack};
use rand_zkvm::isa::{AluOp, Instr, Program, REG_A7};

pub fn hex8(d: &[u32; 8]) -> String { d.iter().map(|w| format!("{w:08x}")).collect() }

pub fn load(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<Program, String> { Program::from_flat_image(bytes).map_err(|e| format!("{e:?}")) }

pub fn disasm(p: &Program) -> Vec<DisasmLine> {
    let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(p.words.len());
    let mut a7: Option<u32> = None;
    for (i, &w) in p.words.iter().enumerate() {
        let pc = p.base_pc.wrapping_add((4 * i) as u32);
        match Instr::decode(w) {
            Ok(instr) => {
                if let Instr::AluImm { op: AluOp::Add, rd, rs1: 0, imm } = instr { if rd == REG_A7 { a7 = Some(imm); } }
                else if let Instr::AluImm { rd, .. } | Instr::AluReg { rd, .. } | Instr::Load { rd, .. } | Instr::Lui { rd, .. } | Instr::Auipc { rd, .. } | Instr::Jal { rd, .. } | Instr::Jalr { rd, .. } = instr { if rd == REG_A7 { a7 = None; } }
                let ecall = if matches!(instr, Instr::Ecall) { a7.and_then(ecall_name).map(str::to_string) } else { None };
                out.push(DisasmLine { pc, word: w, text: text(&instr), ecall });
            }
            Err(e) => out.push(DisasmLine { pc, word: w, text: format!(".word {w:#010x} ; {e:?}"), ecall: None }),
        }
    }
    out
}

pub fn view(bytes: &[u8], cap: usize, op_map: &[OpLine], debug_line: Option<&str>, c_file: &str) -> Result<ImageView, String> {
    let info = pack::describe(bytes).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    let p = load(bytes)?;
    let report = check::check_text(p.base_pc, &p.words, p.words.len(), cap);
    let (source_map, source_map_note) = match debug_line {
        Some(dl) => {
            let rows = parse_debug_line(dl, c_file);
            if rows.is_empty() { (None, Some(format!("the debug build's line table has no rows for {c_file}"))) }
            else { (Some(compose(&rows, op_map)), None) }
        }
        None => (None, Some("no debug build: pc-level source map unavailable; the opcode-to-C map still works".to_string())),
    };
    Ok(ImageView {
        words: p.words.len(), text_words: info.n_text, data_words: info.n_data, prologue_words: p.words.len() - info.n_text,
        base_pc: p.base_pc, text_base: info.text_base,
        hc: hex8(&p.digest()), program_id: hex::encode(chain::program_id(p.base_pc, &p.words)),
        cap_words: cap, fits: p.words.len() <= cap,
        findings: report.findings.iter().map(|f| CheckFinding { addr: f.addr, word: f.word, rule: format!("{:?}", f.rule), what: f.what.clone() }).collect(),
        disasm: disasm(&p), source_map, source_map_note,
    })
}
```

### Task 8: Compact trace: write, read, replay

**Files:**
- Create: `api/crates/terminal-core/src/trace.rs`
- Modify: `api/crates/terminal-core/src/lib.rs`
- Test: `api/crates/terminal-core/tests/trace.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `rand_zkvm::emulator::{execute, Execution, CycleEvent, MemAccess, Syscall, SLOT_W, SPACE_RAM, SPACE_REG}`, `views::{CycleView, SyscallView}`.
- Produces:
  - `trace::write(dir: &Path, exec: &Execution) -> io::Result<u32>` — writes `cycles.bin`, `regwrites.bin`, `memwrites.bin`, `regs.ckpt`, `memckpt.bin`, `memckpt.idx`, `syscalls.json`; returns the cycle count.
  - `trace::Trace::open(dir: &Path) -> io::Result<Trace>` with `total: u32`, `cycles(from: u32, count: u32) -> io::Result<Vec<CycleView>>`, `cycle(clk: u32) -> io::Result<CycleView>`, `regs_at(clk: u32) -> io::Result<[u32; 32]>`, `memory_at(clk: u32, addr: u32, words: u32) -> io::Result<Vec<u32>>`, `syscalls() -> io::Result<Vec<SyscallView>>`.
  - Constants `REG_CKPT = 1024`, `MEM_CKPT = 65_536`, `REC_BYTES = 36`, `MAX_PAGE = 4096`, `MAX_WINDOW = 256`.
  - `trace::syscall_view(clk: u32, s: &Syscall) -> SyscallView`.
- Important: `execute` returns `Err` on a trap with no partial `Execution`, so a trapped run has no trace files; Task 9's summary records the trap instead. (The spec's "debugger jumps to the trap" is therefore "the summary names the trap"; `OutOfCycles(n)` is the one trap whose cycle is known.)

State semantics: `regs_at(clk)` and `memory_at(clk, …)` are the state **before** cycle `clk` executes; `clk == total` is the state after the last cycle; `clk > total` is `InvalidInput`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

`api/crates/terminal-core/tests/trace.rs`:
```rust
#[path = "fixtures.rs"]
mod fixtures;
use rand_zkvm::emulator::{execute, Execution, MemAccess, SPACE_RAM, SPACE_REG};
use rand_zkvm::isa::Program;
use terminal_core::trace::{self, Trace, MEM_CKPT, REG_CKPT};

fn all_accesses(e: &rand_zkvm::emulator::CycleEvent) -> impl Iterator<Item = &MemAccess> {
    e.accesses.iter().chain(e.keccak_accesses.iter()).chain(e.sha256_accesses.iter())
}

/// Every recorded register read equals the replayed register file before that cycle, and every
/// recorded RAM read equals the replayed memory before that cycle.
fn check_replay(t: &Trace, exec: &Execution, clks: impl Iterator<Item = u32>) {
    for clk in clks {
        let e = &exec.events[clk as usize];
        let regs = t.regs_at(clk).unwrap();
        for a in all_accesses(e).filter(|a| a.space == SPACE_REG && !a.is_write) {
            assert_eq!(regs[a.addr as usize], a.value, "clk {clk} reg x{}", a.addr);
        }
        for a in all_accesses(e).filter(|a| a.space == SPACE_RAM && !a.is_write) {
            let w = t.memory_at(clk, a.addr, 1).unwrap();
            assert_eq!(w[0], a.value, "clk {clk} mem {:#x}", a.addr);
        }
        let c = t.cycle(clk).unwrap();
        assert_eq!((c.clk, c.pc, c.next_pc, c.word), (e.clk, e.pc, e.next_pc, e.instr.encode()));
    }
}

#[test]
fn hand_assembled_guests_round_trip() {
    let mut ok = 0;
    for (name, program, inputs) in rand_zkvm::guests::all() {
        let Ok(exec) = execute(&program, &inputs, &[], 1 << 16) else { continue };
        ok += 1;
        let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let total = trace::write(dir.path(), &exec).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(total as usize, exec.cycles(), "{name}");
        let t = Trace::open(dir.path()).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(t.total, total);
        check_replay(&t, &exec, 0..total);
        assert_eq!(t.regs_at(0).unwrap(), [0u32; 32], "{name}");
        assert!(t.regs_at(total).is_ok());
        assert!(t.regs_at(total + 1).is_err());
        assert!(t.memory_at(total + 1, 0, 1).is_err());
        let page = t.cycles(0, 4096).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(page.len(), (total.min(4096)) as usize);
        assert!(t.cycles(total, 10).unwrap().is_empty());
        let sys = t.syscalls().unwrap();
        assert_eq!(sys.len(), exec.events.iter().filter(|e| e.sys.is_some()).count());
        assert!(sys.last().map(|s| s.kind == "halt").unwrap_or(true), "{name}");
    }
    assert!(ok >= 5, "only {ok} guests ran");
}

#[test]
fn erc20_transfer_crosses_both_checkpoint_sizes() {
    let program = Program::from_flat_image(&fixtures::bytes("erc20.image.bin")).unwrap();
    let inputs = fixtures::words("erc20-transfer.words");
    let exec = execute(&program, &inputs, &[], 1 << 18).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(exec.cycles(), 66_235);
    assert!(exec.cycles() as u32 > MEM_CKPT && exec.cycles() as u32 > REG_CKPT);
    let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let total = trace::write(dir.path(), &exec).unwrap();
    let t = Trace::open(dir.path()).unwrap();
    let samples = (0..total).step_by(997)
        .chain([REG_CKPT - 1, REG_CKPT, REG_CKPT + 1, MEM_CKPT - 1, MEM_CKPT, MEM_CKPT + 1, total - 1]);
    check_replay(&t, &exec, samples);
    // a window straddles the sparse map and the write log
    let w = t.memory_at(total, 0x4000, 256).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(w.len(), 256);
    // the cycle records flag what they should
    let halt = t.cycle(total - 1).unwrap();
    assert!(halt.syscall);
    let page = t.cycles(total - 5, 100).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(page.len(), 5);
    assert_eq!(page[4].clk, total - 1);
}

#[test]
fn page_and_window_limits() {
    let (_, program, inputs) = rand_zkvm::guests::all().into_iter().next().unwrap();
    let exec = execute(&program, &inputs, &[], 1 << 16).unwrap();
    let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    trace::write(dir.path(), &exec).unwrap();
    let t = Trace::open(dir.path()).unwrap();
    assert!(t.cycles(0, 4097).is_err());
    assert!(t.memory_at(0, 0, 257).is_err());
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `trace.rs`**

```rust
//! The compact on-disk trace. `rand_zkvm`'s `Execution` holds a `Vec` per cycle (hundreds of MB
//! for SPL Token's 766 k cycles), so a run is folded once into fixed-width files the debugger
//! pages through:
//!
//! * `cycles.bin`     36 B per cycle: clk, pc, next_pc, word, rd, flags, n_alu, rd_val, mem_addr, mem_val, sys_idx
//! * `regwrites.bin`  12 B per register write: clk, reg, value (x0 never recorded)
//! * `memwrites.bin`  12 B per RAM write: clk, word address, value
//! * `regs.ckpt`      32 × u32 every REG_CKPT cycles: the file before that cycle
//! * `memckpt.bin`    every MEM_CKPT cycles: u32 n, then n × (addr, value); `memckpt.idx` is u64 offsets
//! * `syscalls.json`  Vec<SyscallView>
//!
//! All integers little-endian. State at `clk` is the state before cycle `clk` runs.
use crate::views::{CycleView, SyscallView};
use rand_zkvm::emulator::{Execution, MemAccess, Syscall, SPACE_RAM, SPACE_REG};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;
use std::fs::File;
use std::io::{self, BufWriter, Write};
use std::os::unix::fs::FileExt;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};

pub const REG_CKPT: u32 = 1024;
pub const MEM_CKPT: u32 = 65_536;
pub const REC_BYTES: u64 = 36;
pub const LOG_BYTES: u64 = 12;
pub const MAX_PAGE: u32 = 4096;
pub const MAX_WINDOW: u32 = 256;

const F_WRITES_RD: u8 = 1;
const F_MEM_READ: u8 = 2;
const F_MEM_WRITE: u8 = 4;
const F_SYSCALL: u8 = 8;
const F_HASH_ROW: u8 = 16;

pub fn syscall_view(clk: u32, s: &Syscall) -> SyscallView {
    let (kind, detail) = match *s {
        Syscall::Halt => ("halt", String::new()),
        Syscall::WriteOutput { slot, word } => ("write_output", format!("out[{slot}] = {word}")),
        Syscall::ReadInput { idx, word } => ("read_input", format!("in[{idx}] → {word}")),
        Syscall::ReadPublic { idx, word } => ("read_public", format!("pub[{idx}] → {word}")),
        Syscall::Poseidon2 { ptr, n } => ("poseidon2", format!("{n} words at {ptr:#x}")),
        Syscall::Poseidon2Len { ptr, n } => ("poseidon2_len", format!("{n} words at {ptr:#x}")),
        Syscall::Keccak { ptr } => ("keccak", format!("state at {ptr:#x}")),
        Syscall::Sha256 { ptr } => ("sha256", format!("block at {ptr:#x}")),
    };
    SyscallView { clk, kind: kind.into(), detail }
}

fn all_accesses(e: &rand_zkvm::emulator::CycleEvent) -> impl Iterator<Item = &MemAccess> {
    e.accesses.iter().chain(e.keccak_accesses.iter()).chain(e.sha256_accesses.iter())
}

fn put(w: &mut impl Write, v: u32) -> io::Result<()> { w.write_all(&v.to_le_bytes()) }

pub fn write(dir: &Path, exec: &Execution) -> io::Result<u32> {
    std::fs::create_dir_all(dir)?;
    let mut cycles = BufWriter::new(File::create(dir.join("cycles.bin"))?);
    let mut regw = BufWriter::new(File::create(dir.join("regwrites.bin"))?);
    let mut memw = BufWriter::new(File::create(dir.join("memwrites.bin"))?);
    let mut rck = BufWriter::new(File::create(dir.join("regs.ckpt"))?);
    let mut mck = BufWriter::new(File::create(dir.join("memckpt.bin"))?);
    let mut idx: Vec<u64> = Vec::new();
    let mut mck_off: u64 = 0;
    let mut regs = [0u32; 32];
    let mut mem: BTreeMap<u32, u32> = BTreeMap::new();
    let mut syscalls: Vec<SyscallView> = Vec::new();
    for (i, e) in exec.events.iter().enumerate() {
        let clk = i as u32;
        debug_assert_eq!(e.clk, clk);
        if clk % REG_CKPT == 0 { for r in regs { put(&mut rck, r)?; } }
        if clk % MEM_CKPT == 0 {
            idx.push(mck_off);
            put(&mut mck, mem.len() as u32)?;
            for (&a, &v) in &mem { put(&mut mck, a)?; put(&mut mck, v)?; }
            mck_off += 4 + 8 * mem.len() as u64;
        }
        let rdw = e.accesses.iter().find(|a| a.space == SPACE_REG && a.is_write);
        let (rd, rd_val, writes_rd) = match rdw { Some(a) => (a.addr as u8, a.value, true), None => (e.dec.rd as u8, 0, false) };
        let mut flags = 0u8;
        if writes_rd { flags |= F_WRITES_RD; }
        if e.dec.is_lb | e.dec.is_lh | e.dec.is_lw == 1 { flags |= F_MEM_READ; }
        if e.dec.is_sb | e.dec.is_sh | e.dec.is_sw == 1 { flags |= F_MEM_WRITE; }
        if e.sys.is_some() { flags |= F_SYSCALL; }
        if e.hash_row.is_some() { flags |= F_HASH_ROW; }
        let sys_idx = match &e.sys { Some(s) => { syscalls.push(syscall_view(clk, s)); (syscalls.len() - 1) as u32 } None => u32::MAX };
        put(&mut cycles, clk)?; put(&mut cycles, e.pc)?; put(&mut cycles, e.next_pc)?; put(&mut cycles, e.instr.encode())?;
        cycles.write_all(&[rd, flags])?; cycles.write_all(&(e.alu.len().min(u16::MAX as usize) as u16).to_le_bytes())?;
        put(&mut cycles, rd_val)?; put(&mut cycles, e.mem_addr)?; put(&mut cycles, e.mem_val)?; put(&mut cycles, sys_idx)?;
        for a in all_accesses(e).filter(|a| a.is_write) {
            if a.space == SPACE_REG {
                if a.addr == 0 { continue; }
                regs[a.addr as usize] = a.value;
                put(&mut regw, clk)?; put(&mut regw, a.addr)?; put(&mut regw, a.value)?;
            } else if a.space == SPACE_RAM {
                mem.insert(a.addr, a.value);
                put(&mut memw, clk)?; put(&mut memw, a.addr)?; put(&mut memw, a.value)?;
            }
        }
    }
    cycles.flush()?; regw.flush()?; memw.flush()?; rck.flush()?; mck.flush()?;
    let mut idxf = BufWriter::new(File::create(dir.join("memckpt.idx"))?);
    for o in idx { idxf.write_all(&o.to_le_bytes())?; }
    idxf.flush()?;
    std::fs::write(dir.join("syscalls.json"), serde_json::to_vec(&syscalls)?)?;
    Ok(exec.events.len() as u32)
}

pub struct Trace {
    dir: PathBuf,
    pub total: u32,
    cycles: File,
    regwrites: File,
    memwrites: File,
    regs_ckpt: File,
    memckpt: File,
    memckpt_idx: Vec<u64>,
}

fn bad(msg: String) -> io::Error { io::Error::new(io::ErrorKind::InvalidInput, msg) }
fn u32_at(b: &[u8], i: usize) -> u32 { u32::from_le_bytes([b[i], b[i + 1], b[i + 2], b[i + 3]]) }

/// `(clk, key, value)` records of a 12-byte log, sorted by clk. `from..to` by record index.
fn read_log(f: &File, from: u64, to: u64) -> io::Result<Vec<(u32, u32, u32)>> {
    let mut buf = vec![0u8; ((to - from) * LOG_BYTES) as usize];
    f.read_exact_at(&mut buf, from * LOG_BYTES)?;
    Ok(buf.chunks_exact(12).map(|c| (u32_at(c, 0), u32_at(c, 4), u32_at(c, 8))).collect())
}

/// The first record index whose clk is ≥ `clk` (binary search over positional reads).
fn lower_bound(f: &File, n: u64, clk: u32) -> io::Result<u64> {
    let (mut lo, mut hi) = (0u64, n);
    let mut b = [0u8; 4];
    while lo < hi {
        let mid = (lo + hi) / 2;
        f.read_exact_at(&mut b, mid * LOG_BYTES)?;
        if u32::from_le_bytes(b) < clk { lo = mid + 1; } else { hi = mid; }
    }
    Ok(lo)
}

impl Trace {
    pub fn open(dir: &Path) -> io::Result<Trace> {
        let cycles = File::open(dir.join("cycles.bin"))?;
        let total = (cycles.metadata()?.len() / REC_BYTES) as u32;
        let idx_bytes = std::fs::read(dir.join("memckpt.idx"))?;
        let memckpt_idx = idx_bytes.chunks_exact(8).map(|c| u64::from_le_bytes(c.try_into().unwrap())).collect();
        Ok(Trace {
            dir: dir.to_path_buf(), total, cycles,
            regwrites: File::open(dir.join("regwrites.bin"))?,
            memwrites: File::open(dir.join("memwrites.bin"))?,
            regs_ckpt: File::open(dir.join("regs.ckpt"))?,
            memckpt: File::open(dir.join("memckpt.bin"))?,
            memckpt_idx,
        })
    }

    fn log_len(f: &File) -> io::Result<u64> { Ok(f.metadata()?.len() / LOG_BYTES) }

    pub fn syscalls(&self) -> io::Result<Vec<SyscallView>> {
        Ok(serde_json::from_slice(&std::fs::read(self.dir.join("syscalls.json"))?)?)
    }

    pub fn cycles(&self, from: u32, count: u32) -> io::Result<Vec<CycleView>> {
        if count > MAX_PAGE { return Err(bad(format!("count {count} exceeds {MAX_PAGE}"))); }
        if from >= self.total { return Ok(Vec::new()); }
        let n = count.min(self.total - from) as u64;
        let mut buf = vec![0u8; (n * REC_BYTES) as usize];
        self.cycles.read_exact_at(&mut buf, from as u64 * REC_BYTES)?;
        Ok(buf.chunks_exact(REC_BYTES as usize).map(|c| {
            let flags = c[17];
            CycleView {
                clk: u32_at(c, 0), pc: u32_at(c, 4), next_pc: u32_at(c, 8), word: u32_at(c, 12),
                rd: c[16], writes_rd: flags & F_WRITES_RD != 0, rd_val: u32_at(c, 20),
                mem_read: flags & F_MEM_READ != 0, mem_write: flags & F_MEM_WRITE != 0, mem_addr: u32_at(c, 24), mem_val: u32_at(c, 28),
                syscall: flags & F_SYSCALL != 0, hash_row: flags & F_HASH_ROW != 0,
                n_alu: u16::from_le_bytes([c[18], c[19]]), sys_idx: u32_at(c, 32),
            }
        }).collect())
    }

    pub fn cycle(&self, clk: u32) -> io::Result<CycleView> {
        self.cycles(clk, 1)?.into_iter().next().ok_or_else(|| bad(format!("cycle {clk} is past the end ({})", self.total)))
    }

    pub fn regs_at(&self, clk: u32) -> io::Result<[u32; 32]> {
        if clk > self.total { return Err(bad(format!("clk {clk} > total {}", self.total))); }
        let ck = clk / REG_CKPT;
        let mut buf = [0u8; 128];
        self.regs_ckpt.read_exact_at(&mut buf, ck as u64 * 128)?;
        let mut regs = [0u32; 32];
        for (i, r) in regs.iter_mut().enumerate() { *r = u32_at(&buf, 4 * i); }
        let n = Self::log_len(&self.regwrites)?;
        let from = lower_bound(&self.regwrites, n, ck * REG_CKPT)?;
        let to = lower_bound(&self.regwrites, n, clk)?;
        for (_, reg, val) in read_log(&self.regwrites, from, to)? { if reg != 0 && reg < 32 { regs[reg as usize] = val; } }
        Ok(regs)
    }

    pub fn memory_at(&self, clk: u32, addr: u32, words: u32) -> io::Result<Vec<u32>> {
        if words > MAX_WINDOW { return Err(bad(format!("window {words} exceeds {MAX_WINDOW}"))); }
        if clk > self.total { return Err(bad(format!("clk {clk} > total {}", self.total))); }
        let ck = (clk / MEM_CKPT) as usize;
        let off = *self.memckpt_idx.get(ck).ok_or_else(|| bad("missing memory checkpoint".into()))?;
        let mut nb = [0u8; 4];
        self.memckpt.read_exact_at(&mut nb, off)?;
        let n = u32::from_le_bytes(nb) as usize;
        let mut buf = vec![0u8; 8 * n];
        self.memckpt.read_exact_at(&mut buf, off + 4)?;
        let end = addr.saturating_add(words);
        let mut out = vec![0u32; words as usize];
        for c in buf.chunks_exact(8) {
            let a = u32_at(c, 0);
            if a >= addr && a < end { out[(a - addr) as usize] = u32_at(c, 4); }
        }
        let total = Self::log_len(&self.memwrites)?;
        let from = lower_bound(&self.memwrites, total, ck as u32 * MEM_CKPT)?;
        let to = lower_bound(&self.memwrites, total, clk)?;
        for (_, a, v) in read_log(&self.memwrites, from, to)? { if a >= addr && a < end { out[(a - addr) as usize] = v; } }
        Ok(out)
    }
}
```

`Decoded`'s `is_lb` etc. are `u32` flags (0/1), so the `|` expressions above compare to `1`.

Add `pub mod trace;` to `lib.rs`.

```rust
#[test]
fn erc20_transfer_lands_in_tier_18_like_rand_guest_run() {
    let program = Program::from_flat_image(&fixtures::bytes("erc20.image.bin")).unwrap();
    let inputs = fixtures::words("erc20-transfer.words");
    let exec = execute(&program, &inputs, &[], 1 << 20).unwrap();
    let r = rows(&program, inputs.len(), 0, &exec);
    assert_eq!(r.cpu, 66_235);
    assert_eq!(r.digest_rows, program.digest_rows() + rand_zkvm::hash::input_digest_row_count(inputs.len()) + rand_zkvm::hash::public_digest_row_count(0));
    assert!(r.memory > r.cpu, "every cycle has at least one access");
    assert!(r.alu > 0 && r.poseidon2_permutations >= r.digest_rows);
    assert_eq!(pick(&r, cycles_total(&r)), Some(18), "run-transfer.txt says tier 18");
    let caps = capacities(&r, cycles_total(&r));
    assert_eq!(caps.iter().map(|c| c.tier).collect::<Vec<_>>(), vec![10, 12, 14, 16, 18, 20]);
    assert_eq!(caps.iter().map(|c| c.fits).collect::<Vec<_>>(), vec![false, false, false, false, true, true]);
```

`tiers.rs`:
```rust
//! How a run fills the STARK tables and which tier it lands in. Mirrors `Machine::prove_salted_with`
//! and `rand-guest run` term for term; the numbers here are what the tables panel draws.
use crate::views::{TableRows, TierCapacity};
use rand_zkvm::emulator::{Execution, HashRow};
use rand_zkvm::hash::{input_digest_row_count, public_digest_row_count};
use rand_zkvm::isa::Program;
use rand_zkvm::machine::{Tier, MAX_MEM_LOG_HEIGHT, TIERS};

/// Rows of the Poseidon2 table one permutation occupies (`tables::poseidon2::BLOCK`).
pub const POSEIDON2_BLOCK: usize = 32;
/// Rows per Keccak permutation and per SHA-256 compression (`tables::{keccak,sha256}::BLOCK`).
pub const KECCAK_BLOCK: usize = 32;
pub const SHA256_BLOCK: usize = 64;

pub fn rows(program: &Program, n_in: usize, n_pub: usize, exec: &Execution) -> TableRows {
    let digest_rows = program.digest_rows() + input_digest_row_count(n_in) + public_digest_row_count(n_pub);
    let absorb = exec.events.iter().filter(|e| matches!(e.hash_row, Some(HashRow::Absorb { .. }))).count();
    TableRows {
        cpu: exec.cycles(),
        memory: exec.events.iter().map(|e| e.accesses.len() + e.keccak_accesses.len() + e.sha256_accesses.len()).sum(),
        alu: exec.events.iter().map(|e| e.alu.len()).sum(),
        poseidon2_permutations: digest_rows + absorb,
        keccak_permutations: exec.events.iter().filter(|e| e.keccak_row.is_some()).count(),
        sha256_compressions: exec.events.iter().filter(|e| e.sha256_row.is_some()).count(),
        digest_rows,
    }
}

pub fn cycles_total(rows: &TableRows) -> usize { rows.cpu + rows.digest_rows }

pub fn pick(rows: &TableRows, cycles_total: usize) -> Option<usize> {
    Tier::for_workload(cycles_total, rows.poseidon2_permutations).map(|t| t.0)
}

pub fn capacities(rows: &TableRows, cycles_total: usize) -> Vec<TierCapacity> {
    TIERS.iter().map(|&t| {
        let tier = Tier(t);
        let poseidon2_permutations = tier.poseidon2_height() / POSEIDON2_BLOCK;
        let keccak_permutations = (1usize << tier.max_keccak_log_height()) / KECCAK_BLOCK;
        let sha256_compressions = (1usize << tier.max_sha256_log_height()) / SHA256_BLOCK;
        let memory_max = 1usize << MAX_MEM_LOG_HEIGHT;
        let fits = cycles_total <= tier.max_cycles()
            && rows.poseidon2_permutations <= poseidon2_permutations
            && rows.alu <= tier.alu_height()
            && rows.memory <= memory_max
            && rows.keccak_permutations <= keccak_permutations
            && rows.sha256_compressions <= sha256_compressions;
        TierCapacity {
            tier: t, cpu: tier.cpu_height(), alu: tier.alu_height(),
            memory_min: 1usize << tier.min_mem_log_height(), memory_max,
            poseidon2_permutations, keccak_permutations, sha256_compressions, fits,
        }
    }).collect()
}
```

If `rand_zkvm::tables::poseidon2::BLOCK` is public, use it instead of the local constant (and the keccak/sha256 ones likewise); the test does not care which.

```rust
use crate::tiers;
use crate::views::{DiscoveryStep, RunSummary, TableRows};
use rand_zkvm::emulator::ExecError;
use rand_zkvm::isa::Program;

pub fn summarize(rid: &str, program: &Program, n_in: usize, n_pub: usize, exec: &Execution, discovery: Vec<DiscoveryStep>) -> RunSummary {
    let rows = tiers::rows(program, n_in, n_pub, exec);
    let total = tiers::cycles_total(&rows);
    let mut counts: BTreeMap<String, usize> = BTreeMap::new();
    let mut hits: std::collections::HashMap<u32, usize> = std::collections::HashMap::new();
    for e in &exec.events {
        if let Some(s) = &e.sys { *counts.entry(syscall_view(0, s).kind).or_default() += 1; }
        *hits.entry(e.pc).or_default() += 1;
    }
    let mut hot: Vec<(u32, usize)> = hits.into_iter().collect();
    hot.sort_by(|a, b| b.1.cmp(&a.1).then(a.0.cmp(&b.0)));
    hot.truncate(32);
    RunSummary {
        rid: rid.into(), cycles: exec.cycles(), halted: exec.halted, trap: None, trap_clk: None,
        outputs: exec.outputs, status: exec.outputs[0], tier: tiers::pick(&rows, total),
        tiers: tiers::capacities(&rows, total), rows,
        syscall_counts: counts.into_iter().collect(), hot_pcs: hot,
        input_words: n_in, public_words: n_pub, discovery,
    }
}

pub fn trapped(rid: &str, err: &ExecError, n_in: usize, n_pub: usize, discovery: Vec<DiscoveryStep>) -> RunSummary {
    let rows = TableRows { cpu: 0, memory: 0, alu: 0, poseidon2_permutations: 0, keccak_permutations: 0, sha256_compressions: 0, digest_rows: 0 };
    RunSummary {
        rid: rid.into(), cycles: 0, halted: false, trap: Some(format!("{err:?}")),
        trap_clk: match err { ExecError::OutOfCycles(n) => Some(*n as u32), _ => None },
        outputs: [0; 8], status: 2, tier: None, tiers: tiers::capacities(&rows, 0), rows,
        syscall_counts: vec![], hot_pcs: vec![], input_words: n_in, public_words: n_pub, discovery,
    }
}
```

### Task 10: EVM call form, ABI encoding and witness discovery

**Files:**
- Create: `api/crates/terminal-core/src/evm_call.rs`
- Modify: `api/crates/terminal-core/src/lib.rs`
- Test: `api/crates/terminal-core/tests/evm_call.rs`

**Background the implementer needs:** the guest reads storage only through Merkle witnesses supplied in its input (`EvmCall::touched`, at most `evm_core::storage::MAX_WITNESSES = 16`, no two sharing a leaf index `rand_zkvm::evm::slot_index`). A slot read without a witness halts with `Halt::NoWitness`, which does **not** say which slot (`StorageTree::find` compares slots directly). Users must never write witnesses, so this task discovers them by dry-running the host interpreter (`evm_core::abi::run_call_with`, microseconds per run) under a `Host` that records every Keccak permutation:

1. Start with `touched` = the slots the user seeded.
2. Run. If the halt is not `NoWitness`, stop.
3. Otherwise pick the next candidate: the output of the **most recent** 64-byte Keccak message not yet in `touched` (Solidity's mapping slot `keccak256(key ‖ base)`), else the lowest constant slot `0..16` not yet tried. Add it, record a `DiscoveryStep`, go to 2.
4. Stop with an error after 16 witnesses or 32 runs.
5. Prune: drop each discovered (not user-seeded) slot whose removal still gives the same outcome, so the input carries only slots the call touches.

A 64-byte message is recognisable in the permutation input: Keccak-256 pads with byte `0x01` at offset 64 and `0x80` in byte 135 of the 136-byte rate. The state is 50 `u32` words (25 lanes, little-endian), so byte `k` of the message is byte `k % 4` of word `k / 4`. The hash is the first 32 bytes of the output state, read big-endian into `U256` with `U256::from_be_bytes`.

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `rand_zkvm::evm::{EvmCall, SparseTree, HostRef, abi_call, selector, mapping_slot, mapping_slot2, slot_index, TOKEN, ALICE, BOB, SLOT_BALANCES}`, `evm_core::{Host, u256::U256, abi::{run_call_with, Workspace}, interp::{Halt, Outcome}}`, `views::{EvmCallForm, StorageSeed, DiscoveryStep}`.
- Produces:
  - `evm_call::parse_u256(s: &str) -> Result<U256, String>` (decimal or `0x` hex, ≤ 256 bits)
  - `evm_call::encode_calldata(sig: &str, args: &[String]) -> Result<Vec<u8>, String>`
  - `evm_call::build(code: &[u8], form: &EvmCallForm) -> Result<(EvmCall, Vec<DiscoveryStep>), String>` — returns the call with `touched` discovered
  - `evm_call::erc20_transfer_form() -> EvmCallForm` — the ERC-20 sample's default call (ALICE sends BOB 250 of her 1 000)
  - `evm_call::u256_hex(v: &U256) -> String` (`0x` + 64 hex digits)

```rust
#[path = "fixtures.rs"]
mod fixtures;
use evm_core::u256::U256;
use rand_zkvm::evm::{abi_call, erc20_transfer, mapping_slot, ALICE, BOB};
use terminal_core::evm_call::{build, encode_calldata, erc20_transfer_form, parse_u256, u256_hex};
use terminal_core::views::{EvmCallForm, StorageSeed};

#[test]
fn parses_numbers() {
    assert_eq!(parse_u256("250").unwrap(), U256::from_u32(250));
    assert_eq!(parse_u256("0xfa").unwrap(), U256::from_u32(250));
    assert_eq!(parse_u256("1000000000000000000").unwrap(), U256::from_u64(1_000_000_000_000_000_000));
    assert!(parse_u256("").is_err());
    assert!(parse_u256("12a").is_err());
    assert!(parse_u256(&format!("0x1{}", "0".repeat(64))).is_err(), "257 bits");
    assert!(parse_u256("115792089237316195423570985008687907853269984665640564039457584007913129639936").is_err(), "2^256");
}

#[test]
fn encodes_like_abi_call_for_static_types() {
    let bob = u256_hex(&BOB);
    let got = encode_calldata("transfer(address,uint256)", &[bob, "250".into()]).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(got, abi_call("transfer(address,uint256)", &[BOB, U256::from_u32(250)]));
    let got = encode_calldata("f(bool,bytes4,int8)", &["true".into(), "0xdeadbeef".into(), "-1".into()]).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(&got[4 + 31..4 + 32], &[1]);
    assert_eq!(&got[4 + 32..4 + 36], &[0xde, 0xad, 0xbe, 0xef]);
    assert!(got[4 + 64..4 + 96].iter().all(|&b| b == 0xff), "int8 -1 is sign-extended");
}

#[test]
fn refuses_dynamic_types_by_name() {
    for sig in ["f(string)", "f(bytes)", "f(uint256[])", "f(uint256[2])", "f((uint256,bool))"] {
        let e = encode_calldata(sig, &["x".into()]).unwrap_err();
        assert!(e.contains("not supported"), "{sig}: {e}");
    }
    assert!(encode_calldata("f(uint256)", &[]).unwrap_err().contains("expects 1"));
    assert!(encode_calldata("f(address)", &["0x1234".into()]).is_ok(), "short addresses are left-padded");
    assert!(encode_calldata("f(address)", &[format!("0x{}", "1".repeat(42))]).is_err(), "21 bytes");
    assert!(encode_calldata("f(uint8)", &["256".into()]).is_err());
}

#[test]
fn discovers_the_erc20_transfer_witnesses() {
    let code = fixtures::erc20_code();
    let form = erc20_transfer_form();
    let (call, steps) = build(&code, &form).unwrap();
    let want = erc20_transfer(ALICE, BOB, U256::from_u32(250), &[(ALICE, U256::from_u32(1000))]);
    let mut got_touched = call.touched.clone();
    let mut want_touched = want.touched.clone();
    got_touched.sort_by_key(|s| s.0); want_touched.sort_by_key(|s| s.0);
    assert_eq!(got_touched, want_touched, "discovered {steps:?}");
    // same input words as the reference vector, up to witness order
    assert_eq!(call.calldata, want.calldata);
    assert_eq!(call.tree.root(), want.tree.root());
    // the reference outcome: success
    let (_, outcome, _) = call.expected();
    assert_eq!(outcome.status(), 1);
    assert!(steps.iter().any(|s| s.slot == u256_hex(&mapping_slot(&BOB, 0))), "{steps:?}");
}

#[test]
fn a_revert_is_not_an_error() {
    // ALICE has nothing: transfer reverts after reading her balance.
    let mut form = erc20_transfer_form();
    form.storage.clear();
    let (call, _) = build(&fixtures::erc20_code(), &form).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(call.expected().1.status(), 0);
}

#[test]
fn witness_cap_is_an_error() {
    // A contract that SLOADs keccak-derived slots forever cannot be satisfied in 16 witnesses:
    // loop: PUSH1 0 DUP1 MSTORE ... is overkill; use slots 0..=16 directly: 17 constant SLOADs.
    let mut code = Vec::new();
    for s in 0u8..=16 { code.extend_from_slice(&[0x60, s, 0x54, 0x50]); } // PUSH1 s SLOAD POP
    code.push(0x00);
    let form = EvmCallForm { function: "f()".into(), args: vec![], caller: u256_hex(&ALICE), address: None, value: "0".into(), gas_limit: 1_000_000, storage: vec![] };
    let e = build(&code, &form).unwrap_err();
    assert!(e.contains("16"), "{e}");
}

#[test]
fn seeds_become_storage() {
    let form = EvmCallForm {
        function: "f()".into(), args: vec![], caller: u256_hex(&ALICE), address: None, value: "0".into(), gas_limit: 100_000,
        storage: vec![StorageSeed::Slot { slot: "2".into(), value: "7".into() }],
    };
    let code = vec![0x60, 0x02, 0x54, 0x50, 0x00]; // PUSH1 2 SLOAD POP STOP
    let (call, steps) = build(&code, &form).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(call.touched, vec![U256::from_u32(2)]);
    assert!(steps.is_empty(), "a seeded slot needs no discovery");
}
```
